Experience in Design of IT systems and selection of System 
Integrator (SI) / Managed Service Provider(s) (MSP(s)) :  
The Bidder must have completed following number of IT 
consultancy assignments in India having component of 
Design of IT systems, preparation of RFP and bid process 
management for selection of System Integrator (SI)/ 
Managed Service Provider (MSP), in a large Pvt. Enterprise/ 
Government / PSU/ Autonomous bodies/ multi -lateral 
funding agencies in last five years from the date bid 
submission date : 
▪ One Consultancy assignment of value of at least INR 10 
Crore 
Or 
▪ Two Consultancy assignments of at least INR 5 Crore each.  
Or 
▪ Four Consultancy assignments of at least INR 2.5 Crore 
each.  
Experience in Design of IT systems and selection of System 
Integrator (SI) / Managed Service Provider(s) (MSP(s)) :  
The Bidder must have completed or ongoing projects for following 
number of IT consultancy assignments in India having component 
of Design of IT systems, preparation of RFP and bid process 
management for selection of System Integrator (SI)/ Managed 
Service Provider (MSP), in a large Pvt. Enterprise/ Government / 
PSU/ Autonomou s bodies/ multi -lateral funding agencies:  
 
▪ One Consultancy assignment of value of at least INR 10 Crore; Or  
▪ Two Consultancy assignments of at least INR 5 Crore each; Or  
▪ Four Consultancy assignments of at least INR 2.5 Crore each.  
 
The completed projects must be within the last seven years; and 
ongoing project should be in operation for more than last 2 years 
and 50% of the contract value should have been received as 
payment.

40 Page # 90, clause 
5.18 Sub-contracts 
• The consultant may propose engaging subcontractors to 
perform any portion of the services outlined in this 
agreement. However, any subcontracting shall not relieve the 
consultant of their obligations under this contract.  
• The total value of such sub-contracts shall not exceed 25% 
(Twenty per cent) of the total contract value (including 
taxes). 
• The consultant shall:  
o Secure prior approval from purchaser to engage or change 
any sub -contractor(s). Sub -contracting by the consultant 
without the approval of  the purchaser shall be a breach of 
contract.  
o Mention the details of any sub-contracting proposed in the 
bid, including the name of sub -contractor, activity assigned 
and duration of sub -contract. 
o Ensure that any subcontractors engaged are suitably 
qualified and capable of fulfilling their assigned tasks.  
• Sub-contracting certificate is required from the consultant 
on the annual basis confirming the value of sub-contract and 
compliance that sub -contracting is within the value of 
percentage mention unde r clause 2.  
• All the resources which are not sub -contracted shall be on 
the payroll of consultant. Purchasers reserve the right to seek 
documents to verify the employment.  
• The consultant shall not be allowed to sub -contract the 
services to any consultant from a country which shares a land 
border with India unless such contractor is registered with the 
competent authority  
• The Competent Authority for the purpose of registration 
shall be the Registration Committee constituted by the 
Department for Promot ion of Industry and Internal Trade 
(DPIIT). 
• “Consultant from a country which shares a land border with 
India” means:  
Subject to the restrictions outlined in this RFP document, 
subcontracting may be permitted in exceptional circumstances 
with the prior approval of UIDAI.  
 
The consultant shall remain fully responsible for the 
performance of all contractual obligations, including those 
carried out by any approved subcontractor.  
 
Any sub-contracting undertaken without the prior approval of 
UIDAI shall be considered a breach of contract.

We request change in the clause as 
follows – 
Bidder’s prior experience in implementing 
large-scale Digital Public Infrastructure 
(DPI) projects (e.g.,National ID systems, 
payment platforms, e-governance, etc.) 
Number of successfully completed 
projects in large-scale digital public 
infrastructure serving more than 1 
Cr.active users (in case of DPI having 
individuals as end-users) and more 
than 1 Lakh active users (in case of DPI 
having businesses as end-users) in a year. 
a) Total of 1 project fulfilling either 
criteria = 1 mark 
b) Total of 2 projects fulfilling either 
criteria = 3 marks 
c) Total of ≥ 3 projects fulfilling either 
criteria = 5 marks 
Most Digital Public 
Infrastructure (DPI) and Digital 
Public 
Goods (DPG) programs in India 
are still in the early stages 
of adoption. While many are 
technically complex and 
nationally significant, very few 
have yet reached the scale of 
over 8 crore active users. As 
such, the current scoring 
criterion may unintentionally 
disadvantage capable bidders 
who have successfully worked 
with platforms with meaningful, 
though smaller user bases. 
 
This is particularly relevant for 
DPI platforms that cater to 
businesses as end-users, where 
the scale of active users is 
inherently limited compared to 
citizen-facing systems. 
Adjusting the scoring 
benchmarks to consider projects 
with over 1 crore active users 
for individual-facing DPIs and 
over 10 lakh users for business-
facing ones would still preserve 
evaluation rigor while enabling 
inclusion of relevant projects. 
 
We also request you to allow 
submission of ongoing projects 
with such DPI that meet the 
threshold, as many of these are 
multi-phase long-term projects. 
No Change

142 Page no.: 38 of

Section: 2 
Clause: 3.14 
Sub-Clause: 
3.14.6 
Final Total Score (FTS) = (TS * T) + (FS 
* C) 
It is requested that normalization of 
technical should be done, as is the norm in 
public sector RFPs. 
 
Normalization process for technical 
scores: 
The best technical proposal with highest 
technical score (Tm) will be given a 
technical score (St) of 100 points. The 
technical scores (St) of the other 
Technical Proposals will be determined 
using the following formula: 
[St = 100 x T/Tm, in which St is the 
technical score, Tm is the highest 
technical score, and T is the technical 
score of bidders under consideration]. 
 
Normalization process of financial scores: 
Proposal with lowest cost shall be given a 
“Financial Score” of 100 (Hundred) and 
other proposals shall be given “Financial 
Score” that are inversely proportional to 
their prices w.r.t the lowest offer. 
The “Financial Score” (Sr) of aTenderer 
shall be worked out as per the following 
formula: 
[Sr = 100 x Fm/ F in which Sr is the 
Financial Score of tenderer; Fm is the 
lowest total price received & F the total 
quoted price of the tenderer]. 
Normalization ensures a fair and 
transparent evaluation by 
proportionately aligning all 
technical scores against the 
highest-scoring technical 
proposal. This prevents undue 
advantage to marginally higher-
scoring proposals and maintains 
a balanced weightage between 
technical and financial 
evaluations in Quality- and 
Cost-Based Selection (QCBS) 
frameworks. 
 
This is a norm in all recent 
RFPs for central/ state 
Governments like 
• Engagement of Consulting 
firm for Benchmarking of 
Research and Development 
Activities of HAL with Global 
Aerospace and Defence 
Companies issued in 2025 
• Engagement of Strategy 
Consultant for Operations, 
Marketing, Technology, 
Revenue, Costs and Overall 
business model for NHSRCL 
issued in 2025 
• Services Export Strategy 2030 
RFP issued by Ministry of 
Commerce & Industry in 2024 
• Project Management 
Consultants for Implementation 
of Recommendations of Project 
Studies Executed by Ministry of 
Road Transport and Highways 
No Change

147 Page no.:98 
Section 5.25.2 – 
Payment 
Process 
(Staffbased 
Payment 
Formula) 
MP = R × (1 – L/N) 
where L is number of unpaid leave days 
and N is 
total calendar days in the month. 
MP = R × (1 – L/D) 
Where L is number of unpaid leave days 
and D is working days in the month 
(excluding holidays). 
Calculating attendance based on 
total calendar days, including 
weekends and public holidays, 
does not accurately reflect 
standard working expectations. 
It may result in under-
compensation for resources who 
have contributed fully during 
the month. Moreover, it 
introduces administrative 
complexity in tracking 
availability and effort. 
An industry-aligned approach is 
to use working days per month 
as the denominator for 
calculating man-month effort. 
This reflects typical staffing 
schedules and ensures equitable 
and transparent billing. The 
suggested formula accounts for 
actual working days, avoids 
penalizing resources for 
weekends or non-working days, 
and ensures consistency across 
project months. 
No Change

(inclusive of any resourcing, travel costs,

any resourcing, travel costs, but exclusive

travel costs, but exclusive of any

At this stage, team structures are 
indicative and are likely to 
evolve based on UIDAI’s 
emerging priorities, stakeholder 
inputs, and ongoing refinements 
to the roadmap. The nature of 
the engagement—especially 
across Phases 2 and 3 - will 
require iterative alignment with 
UIDAI’s evolving technology 
architecture, partner ecosystem, 
and policy directions. As such, 
defining fixed unit costs for 
each individual role or named 
resource over a multi-year 
horizon may not accurately 
reflect how actual delivery will 
be structured. 
 
Furthermore, certain roles, such 
as leadership, quality assurance, 
subject matter experts, and 
centralized program 
governance, are often 
distributed across multiple team 
members or shared across 
workstreams. These roles may 
not be easily attributable to a 
single named resource or 
tracked as standalone line items 
in a per-resource permonth 
billing format. In such cases, a 
consolidated view of phase-wise 
cost, especially a fixed price for 
Phase 1 and a time and materials 
model (man-month basis) for 
Phases 2 and 3; provides a more 
No Change

Client shares deliverables and any third-

may use it on a nonreliance basis. This 
would protect us from risks of third-party 
claims based on reliance on the 
deliverables, which are prepared only for 
the Client’s organization. We are not 
willing to assume any legal obligations to 
non clients that could arise from the

169 Page no 28,  
Section 3 - 
Bidding Process 
3.11.3 The pre-
qualification 
criteria, 6 
Turnover 
The bidder must have an average annual 
turnover of minimum INR 500 Crores 
during the last 3 financial years ending 
31st March 2024. (2021-22, 2022-23 and 
2023-24). The turnover refers to the 
turnover of the company and not the 
composite turnover of its subsidiaries / 
sister concerns, etc. 
May we request to reduce the minimum 
average annual turnover as: The bidder 
must have an average annual turnover of 
minimum INR 300 Crores during the last 
3 financial years ending 31st March 2024. 
(2021-22, 2022-23 and 2023-24). 
The turnover refers to the turnover of the 
company and not the composite turnover 
of its subsidiaries / sister concerns, etc. 
As we emphasize the clause 
from the Procurement Manual 
for Consultancy Services (June 
2022), Page No. 78, Clause 
5.2.10, it is noted that while 
selecting consultants, some 
procuring entities are setting the 
minimum qualifying turnover at 
5 to 10 times the estimated cost 
of the consultancy work. This, 
prima facie, appears excessive. 
Higher qualification criteria 
reduce competition, as they are 
more restrictive, and if set 
unduly high, they may increase 
the cost without any 
corresponding improvement in 
quality. It is therefore suggested 
that such criteria be determined 
on a reasonable basis while 
drafting tender documents, and 
higher minimum qualifying 
turnover requirements should be 
included only if they are 
adequately justified. 
No Change

should not be penalized on

pertaining to Phase-2 and/or

Section: 3

Sub- Clause: 
3.12.12 (3) 
Bidder’s prior experience in implementing 
large scale Digital Public Infrastructure 
(DPI) projects (e.g., National ID systems, 
payment 
platforms, e-governance, etc.) 
Number of successfully completed 
projects in large-scale digital public 
infrastructure serving more than 8 Cr. 
active users 
a) 1 project = 1 mark 
b) 2 projects = 3 marks 
c) ≥ 3 projects = 5 marks 
We request change in the clause as 
follows – 
Bidder’s prior experience in implementing 
large-scale Digital Public Infrastructure 
(DPI) projects (e.g., National ID systems, 
payment platforms, e-governance, etc.) 
Number of successfully completed 
projects in large-scale digital public 
infrastructure serving more than 1 Cr. 
active users (in case of DPI having 
individuals as end-users) and more than 1 
Lakh active users (in case of DPI having 
businesses as end-users) in a year 
a) Total of 1 project fulfilling either 
criteria = 1 mark 
b) Total of 2 projects fulfilling either 
criteria = 3 marks 
c) Total of ≥ 3 projects fulfilling either 
criteria = 5 marks 
Most Digital Public 
Infrastructure (DPI) and Digital 
Public Goods (DPG) programs 
in India are still in the early 
stages of adoption. While many 
are technically complex and 
nationally significant, very few 
have yet reached the scale of 
over 8 crore active users. As 
such, the current scoring 
criterion may unintentionally 
disadvantage capable bidders 
who have successfully worked 
with platforms with meaningful, 
though smaller user bases. This 
is particularly relevant for DPI 
platforms that cater to 
businesses as end-users, where 
the scale of active users is 
inherently limited compared to 
citizen-facing systems. 
Adjusting the scoring 
benchmarks to consider projects 
with over 1 crore active users 
for individualfacing DPIs and 
over 10 lakh users for business-
facing ones would still preserve 
evaluation rigor while enabling 
inclusion of relevant projects. 
We also request you to allow 
submission of ongoing projects 
with such DPI that meet the 
threshold, as many of these are 
multi-phase long-term projects. 
Refer Corrigendum

Section: 5 
Clause: 5.22 
Sub-Clause: 
5.22.3 
If at any time during the performance of 
the contract, the consultant encounters 
conditions impeding timely delivery of the 
services / deliverable, the consultant shall 
promptly notify the Purchaser in writing of 
the fact/ reason of the delay, it’s likely 
duration and its cause(s). As soon as 
practicable after receipt of the Consultant's 
notice, the Purchaser shall evaluate the 
situation and may at its discretion extend 
the consultant's time for performance/ 
deliverables, in which case the extension 
shall be ratified by the parties. If the 
consultant request to delay delivery of the 
services is not found acceptable to the 
purchaser, provisions of clause 5.28 would 
be invoked. 
We suggest that a clarification be 
provided that for the extended period of 
time, the Consultant shall be paid on a pro 
rata basis since the delay was caused due 
to circumstances beyond the Consultant’s 
control 
Given the complexity of the 
assignment, multiple parties and 
stakeholders involved at various 
stages, and large number of 
factors at play, there could be 
situation(s) where delays are 
caused due to circumstances 
beyond the Consultant’s control, 
and not attributable to the 
Consultant’s actions or 
omissions. (e.g., period between 
signing of contract with MSP 
and MSP initiating work) For 
such extended period of time, 
where pre-aligned, the 
Consultant would continue to 
deploy resources and perform 
obligations under the 
assignment. Thus, to 
enable fair compensation for 
extended resource deployment, 
we suggest that pro rata 
payments be provided to the 
Consultant during such periods. 
This is in line with similar at 
scale projects in both public 
and private sector, ensuring 
transparency and appropriate 
incentive-sharing across the 
board. This would help ensure 
continuity of deployed resources 
and maintain momentum in 
project delivery. 
No Change

The Bidder must have completed 
following number of IT consultancy 
assignments in India having component of 
Design of IT systems, preparation of RFP 
and bid process management for selection 
of System Integrator (SI)/Managed Service 
Provider (MSP), in a large Pvt. 
Enterprise/Government / 
PSU/Autonomous bodies/ multilateral 
funding agencies in last five years from the 
date bid submission date. 
1. We request that both completed and 
ongoing projects be considered. For 
ongoing projects, a threshold of having 
minimum 40% accrued consultancy fees 
(out of total) from the project, can be 
outlined. 
2. We request that a Chartered Accountant 
(CA certificate for completed and ongoing 
projects be 
considered to demonstrate compliance 
with the stated criteria, both for ongoing 
and completed 
projects. 
1. A large number of such 
projects are large-scale, with 
multiple inter-dependent and 
sequential modules, 
workstreams, deliverables and 
milestones - which are typically 
executed over extended 
durations of time and structured 
into multiple phases. Therefore, 
we request that ongoing projects 
be considered alongside 
completed ones. Ongoing 
projects which have been 
progressed to a reasonable 
extent indisputably demonstrate 
the competencies, expertise and 
implementation know-how 
spanning multiple stage-gates 
towards completion. 
2. Chartered Accountants, with 
their financial expertise and 
oversight, are well-equipped to 
accurately assess project 
progress and completion status. 
Their certification provides 
credible and verifiable evidence 
of project execution status. This 
will be in line with industry 
practices with CA certification 
is required for similar use cases.
